def _persist_session_headers(session_id: str, headers: dict | None) ->
bool:
"""Persist endpoint auth headers for DB-backed session
metadata."""
delays = (0.05, 0.15, 0.35)
last_exc: Exception | None = None
for attempt in range(len(delays) + 1):
db = SessionLocal()
try:
db_session = db.query(DbSession).filter(DbSession.id ==
session_id).first()
if db_session:
db_session.headers = headers or {}
db_session.updated_at = utcnow_naive()
db.commit()
return True
except Exception as exc:
db.rollback()
last_exc = exc
if attempt >= len(delays):
break
if "database is locked" not in str(exc).lower():
break
time.sleep(delays[attempt])
finally:
db.close()
logger.warning(
"Failed to persist headers for session %s; continuing with inmemory headers: %s",
session_id,
last_exc,
)
return False

def _load_session_from_db(self, session_id: str):
"""Hydrate a single session (with messages) from the
database."""
db = SessionLocal()
try:
db_session = db.query(DbSession).filter(DbSession.id ==
session_id).first()
if db_session is None:
raise KeyError(f"Session {session_id} not found")
session = self._db_to_session(db_session, db)
if session:
self.sessions[session_id] = session
else:
# No messages — fall back to metadata-only entry so
callers
# don't crash on KeyError for empty sessions.
meta = self._db_to_session_meta(db_session)
if meta is None:
raise KeyError(f"Session {session_id} could not be
loaded")
self.sessions[session_id] = meta
except KeyError:
raise
except Exception as e:
logger.error(f"Error loading session {session_id}: {e}")
raise
finally:
db.close()

Step 48 — What we can conclude
The session manager serializes and restores DbSession.headers directly, and
_persist_session_headers() writes the supplied dictionary back to the database. There is no
encryption step in these persistence helpers.
However, the DbSession model definition was not found in core/database.py , so we still
cannot establish the column's actual SQLAlchemy type or whether a custom type decorator
protects it. I won't assume it is plaintext based only on these helpers.
The other important point is that session headers are actively restored into memory and reused
by multiple subsystems. We should preserve that existing behaviour for non-Groq sessions.

📦[deck@odysseus-box ~/OdysseusProject]$
Step 50 — Findings
This confirms an important architectural point:
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 478/588
resolve_endpoint() already resolves registered endpoints through
resolve_endpoint_runtime() . Once the shared resolver supports host-mounted provider
keys, this path can use them without storing them in session metadata.
DbSession.headers is a SQLAlchemy JSON column, not an encrypted credential store.
Session headers are copied into memory and persisted by session creation, session
switching, and _persist_session_headers() . They are also restored when sessions are
loaded.
Consequently, we must not put a host-file provider key into sess.headers . Doing so
would copy the credential into the session database as ordinary JSON.
The safe direction is to keep the session's endpoint identity ( endpoint_id , URL and model)
and resolve credentials at request time. Existing custom endpoint and OAuth credentials still
need to work.
Step 51 — Inspect endpoint selection and session switching
Before designing the change, we should establish whether session creation and switching can
retain endpoint identity while avoiding credential persistence. This is a read-only inspection.

===== 1. ENDPOINT-BY-ID RUNTIME RESOLUTION =====
def _resolve_endpoint_by_id_with_descriptor(
ep_id: str,
model: Optional[str] = None,
owner: Optional[str] = None,
*,
require_exact_model: bool = False,
) -> Optional[Tuple[Tuple[str, str, Dict], dict]]:
"""Resolve a concrete endpoint/model plus its non-secret
descriptor.
Returns None if the endpoint doesn't exist or is disabled. Used to
turn
a configured fallback entry ({endpoint_id, model}) into a dispatch
target.
"""
if not ep_id:
return None
db = SessionLocal()
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 480/588
try:
q = db.query(ModelEndpoint).filter(
ModelEndpoint.id == ep_id,
ModelEndpoint.is_enabled == True,
)
if owner:
from src.auth_helpers import owner_filter
q = owner_filter(q, ModelEndpoint, owner)
ep = q.first()
if not ep:
return None
try:
base, api_key = resolve_endpoint_runtime(ep,
owner=owner)
except Exception as e:
logger.warning("Could not resolve endpoint runtime
credentials: %s", e)
return None
chat_url = build_chat_url(base)
headers = build_headers(api_key, base)
m = (model or "").strip()
enabled_models = _endpoint_enabled_models(ep)
if require_exact_model:
# Explicit foreground fallback entries are concrete choices. A
# hidden or known-missing model must disable the entry
instead of
# silently substituting another model from the endpoint.
if not m or m in _endpoint_hidden_models(ep):
return None
if enabled_models and m not in enabled_models:
return None
else:
# Legacy Utility/Vision chains retain their model-repair
behavior.
if m and m in _endpoint_hidden_models(ep):
m = ""
if not m:
m = _first_chat_model(enabled_models) or ""
if not m:
return None
return (
(chat_url, m, headers),
{
"endpoint_id": ep.id,
"endpoint_label": getattr(ep, "name", None) or ep.id,
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 481/588
"endpoint_cost_tracked": endpoint_cost_tracked(
chat_url,
getattr(ep, "endpoint_kind", None),
),
},
)
except Exception as e:
logger.debug(f"Could not resolve endpoint {ep_id}: {e}")
return None
finally:
db.close()
def resolve_endpoint_by_id(
ep_id: str,
model: Optional[str] = None,
owner: Optional[str] = None,
*,
require_exact_model: bool = False,
) -> Optional[Tuple[str, str, Dict]]:
"""Resolve a specific endpoint id (+ optional model) to its
runtime route."""
resolved = _resolve_endpoint_by_id_with_descriptor(
ep_id,
model,
owner=owner,
require_exact_model=require_exact_model,
)
return resolved[0] if resolved else None

def _has_auth_keys(headers) -> bool:
"""True if a headers dict carries an Authorization/x-api-key
entry."""
return isinstance(headers, dict) and any(
k.lower() in ('authorization', 'x-api-key') for k in headers
)
def resolve_session_auth(sess, session_id: str, owner: Optional[str] =
None):

failed refresh.

Step 51 — findings
We have now confirmed an important security issue in the existing session design.
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 491/588
DbSession.headers is a SQLAlchemy JSON column ( core/database.py:214 ). Session
authentication headers are therefore persisted in the database as JSON; they are not using
the EncryptedText mechanism used by ModelEndpoint.api_key .
Session creation and endpoint switching currently copy the registered endpoint's API key
into session.headers , then persist those headers.
resolve_session_auth() also resolves credentials from the endpoint and writes the
resulting headers back into the session row.
The good news: resolve_endpoint_by_id() already uses resolve_endpoint_runtime() .
Once that shared resolver supports host-mounted provider keys, this route can resolve
them without adding another credential store.
Design consequence: host-file provider keys must be resolved at request time and must never
be copied into DbSession.headers . For provider-backed sessions, persist endpoint identity
and model selection, not the provider's authorization header. Existing custom endpoint and
OAuth behavior must be preserved deliberately rather than indiscriminately clearing all session
headers.
One distinction remains to verify before designing the change: whether chat requests can use
freshly resolved headers in memory without persisting them, and whether any other session
consumers depend on the persisted headers.

ChatGPT said:
Step 52 — Session authentication findings
This confirms an important architectural point: resolve_session_auth() is the central
recovery point for normal chat authentication, called by both streaming and non-streaming
chat routes.
The current design still has a security weakness: it writes resolved authentication headers back
into DbSession.headers , which is ordinary, unencrypted JSON. We should not extend that
behaviour to Groq's host-file key.
What the inspection establishes
1. Endpoint identity is already handled properly. resolve_endpoint_by_id() resolves the
exact enabled endpoint, checks ownership, and calls resolve_endpoint_runtime() . That
is a good foundation for host-file credentials.
2. Session creation and switching still use database keys directly. They build headers from
endpoint_row.api_key and persist them. They need to be adjusted so a registered
provider can authenticate without putting its key into session storage.
3. Chat authentication has a recovery mechanism. resolve_session_auth() resolves the
endpoint key when session headers are missing, but currently persists the resulting
headers. That persistence must change for provider-file credentials.
4. There are secondary consumers. Research, memory, history compaction, email, webhook
and agent/session tools also read session headers. We must preserve legitimate custom
headers and avoid assuming every header is a provider API key.
Important distinction
_resolve_endpoint_by_id_with_descriptor() already uses the shared resolver and builds
headers in memory. That path appears compatible with the intended design: resolve
credentials for the exact endpoint when constructing a runtime route, without saving them to
the session.
The remaining challenge is the older session-based path. In particular:
A session may contain legacy credentials already.
Custom endpoint headers must continue working.
Host-file credentials must be resolved at request time.
New host-file credentials must never be written to DbSession.headers .
We should not silently erase existing session data or migrate old credentials without
approval.

📦[deck@odysseus-box ~/OdysseusProject]$
Step 53 — findings
This confirms that session headers are used in more places than the main chat route. We need
to preserve those paths when introducing host-file credentials.
1. What the output establishes
One particularly important detail: session_tools.py deliberately inherits the parent's runtime
headers when a child chat uses the same model. That behavior protects against accidentally
selecting a stale credential. We must preserve that behavior while ensuring host-file keys aren't
stored in the session database.
2. Design consequence
The shared credential resolver must work at the actual provider-request boundary, not just
when a session is created or when the main chat route starts.
The intended distinction is:
Area How session headers are used Implication
Main chat resolve_session_auth() before
requests
Existing central repair point, but only called
by chat routes
Research Passed as fallback to
resolve_endpoint()
Must resolve credentials for the selected
endpoint without persisting them
Memory Passed to resolve_task_endpoint() Same requirement for memory extraction,
audit and import
History compaction Used when no utility endpoint
overrides the session
Must support the session's actual endpoint
credentials
Agent session tools Passed directly to llm_call_async() Potential bypass of a future shared
credential resolver
Research follow-up
sessions
Copies source session headers into a
new session
Must not copy host-file secrets into
persisted session data
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 528/588
Persisted session data: endpoint identity, model, route metadata and safe non-secret
custom settings.
Runtime request: resolve the endpoint's credentials, construct authentication headers, and
pass them to the provider.
Never persist host-file API keys in DbSession.headers .
We should not blindly remove every Authorization or x-api-key header from existing
sessions. Some existing flows may use explicit custom credentials or subscription
authentication. We need to distinguish those cases before changing persistence.
3. Next inspection — Step 54
Before proposing implementation, I want to inspect the current resolve_session_auth() and
resolve_task_endpoint() behavior together. This should tell us whether we can extend an
existing resolver rather than introduce another competing one.

def _has_auth_keys(headers) -> bool:
"""True if a headers dict carries an Authorization/x-api-key
entry."""
return isinstance(headers, dict) and any(
k.lower() in ('authorization', 'x-api-key') for k in headers
)

def _match_cached_model_id(requested: str, models) ->
Optional[str]:
if not requested or not models:
return None
model_ids = [str(m) for m in models if m]
if requested in model_ids:
return requested
req_base = os.path.basename(requested.rstrip("/"))
for model_id in model_ids:
if os.path.basename(model_id.rstrip("/")) == req_base:
return model_id
return None

def _db_to_session(self, db_session: DbSession, db) ->
Optional[Session]:

def _persist_session_headers(session_id: str, headers: dict | None) ->
bool:
"""Persist endpoint auth headers for DB-backed session
metadata."""
delays = (0.05, 0.15, 0.35)
last_exc: Exception | None = None
for attempt in range(len(delays) + 1):
db = SessionLocal()
try:
db_session = db.query(DbSession).filter(DbSession.id ==
session_id).first()
if db_session:
db_session.headers = headers or {}
db_session.updated_at = utcnow_naive()
db.commit()
return True
except Exception as exc:
db.rollback()
last_exc = exc
if attempt >= len(delays):
break
if "database is locked" not in str(exc).lower():
break
time.sleep(delays[attempt])
finally:
db.close()

"Failed to persist headers for session %s; continuing with inmemory headers: %s",
session_id,

📦[deck@odysseus-box ~/OdysseusProject]$
Step 54 — findings
This is an important result: Odysseus already has a shared runtime credential resolver, but the
session-auth path currently defeats the security goal by persisting the resolved headers.
1. The existing resolver is reusable
resolve_endpoint_runtime(ep, owner) already handles:
Static API keys from ModelEndpoint.api_key .
Session-backed credentials through provider_auth_id .
Runtime base-URL normalization.
That is the right foundation for adding host-file credentials. We should extend this function (or
a narrowly scoped helper it calls), rather than create a second independent credential system.
2. The main security problem is confirmed
resolve_session_auth() currently:
1. Finds an enabled endpoint, owner-scoped.
2. Resolves its runtime API key.
3. Builds authentication headers.
4. Assigns those headers to sess.headers .
5. Writes the headers into DbSession.headers and commits.
That last step is incompatible with the host-key requirement. DbSession.headers is ordinary
JSON, not encrypted.
The fix must make host-file authentication request-local and prevent it from being written back
to the database. We must also preserve the existing endpoint identity and owner checks.
3. There is a second important boundary
resolve_endpoint() returns fallback_headers unchanged when it uses the caller's fallback
session endpoint. That means simply adding host-file support to
resolve_endpoint_runtime() will not automatically cover every task, research, memory or
history request.
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 544/588
We need to resolve credentials when a request uses a registered endpoint, including when that
endpoint is supplied as a session fallback.
4. What must remain intact
Existing OAuth/session-backed authentication.
Explicit custom endpoint credentials.
Owner-scoped endpoint selection.
Session-specific route identity.
Parent-to-child agent session runtime inheritance.
Custom non-authentication headers.
Existing local endpoint behavior.
We should not indiscriminately clear every session header or migrate existing stored credentials
without first identifying their origin.
Step 55 — final resolver-boundary inspection
One last read-only check will clarify how resolve_endpoint() validates the selected model
and returns its headers, and whether session creation already preserves the endpoint ID
needed for reliable credential lookup.

Step 56 — discovery complete
We now have enough information to design the integration without guessing at the important
credential paths.
The most important finding from Step 55 is that Odysseus already stores a stable
endpoint_id in each session. That gives us a much safer way to retrieve credentials than
matching URLs or searching for an endpoint with the same provider.
1. Proposed architecture
Steam Deck host
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 554/588
/home/deck/.config/odysseus/providers/groq/odysseus-groq.key
Owned by deck · permissions 600 · outside Git
Mounted into odysseus-box
Shared provider credential resolver
Provider identity + endpoint ID + owner checks
Chat requests
Temporary auth headers
Background tasks
Research, memory, tools
Model discovery
Separate from authentication
Connection testing
Independent health result
Provider API — Groq first
No automatic fallback or provider switching
2. Session credential protection
This is the critical security correction.
DbSession.headers is ordinary JSON. We should therefore stop treating it as a credential
store.
The resolver should use the session's exact endpoint_id and owner scope. It should not guess
from a matching URL or borrow credentials from another endpoint.
Data Where it belongs
Endpoint ID, URL and model Session database
Host-file provider API key Host key file
Authentication headers Runtime memory, only when making a request
Non-secret session metadata Session database
Legacy custom headers Preserve existing behaviour until individually reviewed
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 555/588
One additional concern: existing sessions may already contain API keys in their JSON headers.
We must not silently erase or migrate them. That requires a separate, backed-up and verified
migration decision.
3. Seven-provider foundation
The common architecture should support these providers from the beginning:
Only Groq gets implemented and tested first. The other six receive no keys or active
integrations during this first implementation.
4. Connection status semantics
Model-list discovery must never turn an authentication failure into ONLINE. Cached or curated
models are not proof of connectivity. Web search/grounding availability remains a separate
capability.

Gemini odysseus-gemini.key

10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 556/588
5. Proposed implementation sequence
1. Host key foundation: create the Groq key directory/file safely, with restrictive permissions
and no key displayed in terminal output.
2. Shared credential resolver: read provider key files and preserve existing database/OAuth
credential behaviour.
3. Session protection: prevent host-file credentials from entering DbSession.headers ;
resolve credentials at request time.
4. Runtime integration: connect chat, session switching, research, memory, background tasks
and other confirmed provider request paths.
5. Groq connection test: implement independent authenticated testing and correct status
classification.
6. Provider UI: show connection status, active provider/model, Test Connection and sanitized
logs without exposing credentials.
7. Verification: focused tests, regression tests, restart/status checks and secret-leak checks.
Each stage will be inspected and verified before moving on. No broad frontend rewrite.
Decision before implementation
There is one important scope choice to settle: existing API keys supplied through Odysseus's
normal endpoint form can currently be stored in encrypted ModelEndpoint.api_key , while
some session paths copy them into unencrypted session JSON.
My recommendation is:
Use host key files for the seven managed providers, keep existing encrypted database
credentials working for other endpoints, and stop persisting authentication headers in
session JSON. Preserve legacy session credentials until we have a controlled migration plan; do
not silently delete them.
This gives us the new security architecture without unexpectedly breaking existing custom
endpoints or OAuth integrations.
If you approve this architecture, say “LET'S GO”. We will begin with Step 1 only: the Groq
host-key directory and permission checks. No API key needs to be pasted into this
conversation.

Run this in Konsole or your existing SSH session on the Steam Deck host, not inside odysseusbox .
The script:
Creates the recovery directory if it doesn't exist.
Refuses to overwrite an existing document.
Writes the Markdown document in one operation.
Does not modify the Odysseus project.
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail
RECOVERY_DIR="/home/deck/OdysseusRecovery"
FILE="$RECOVERY_DIR/ODYSSEUS_PROVIDER_INTEGRATION_ARCHITECTURE.md"
echo "===== ODYSSEUS PROVIDER ARCHITECTURE DOCUMENT ====="
mkdir -p "$RECOVERY_DIR"
if [[ -e "$FILE" ]]; then
echo "ABORTED: The document already exists."
echo "Existing file: $FILE"
echo "Nothing was overwritten."
exit 1
fi
cat > "$FILE" <<'ODYSSEUS_DOCUMENT'
# ODYSSEUS — Provider Integration Architecture

## 1. Mission and architectural objective
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 560/588
This document records the findings, constraints, security decisions, and proposed
architecture resulting from the 56-step investigation of the existing Odysseus
codebase.
The objective is to integrate multiple cloud AI providers into Odysseus without
compromising its existing architecture, credential security, session handling, or
local functionality.
Groq is the first implementation target.
However, the architecture must be designed once and support seven providers:
1. Groq
2. Cloudflare
3. OpenAI
4. Gemini
5. Mistral
6. Cerebras
7. OpenRouter
The intention is not to build seven independent integrations.
The intention is to build one shared provider architecture, then onboard each
provider through its configuration and provider-specific transport requirements.
### Core principles
- Diagnose before modifying.
- Reuse existing architecture wherever possible.
- Centralize credential resolution.
- Keep provider secrets outside the project repository.
- Never persist host-file credentials in unencrypted session data.
- Preserve existing custom endpoint and OAuth functionality.
- Keep provider selection manual.
- Never automatically switch providers.
- Never automatically consume paid API allowance.
- Keep model discovery separate from connection authentication.
- Keep API availability separate from web-search or grounding availability.
- Preserve existing local providers and functionality.
- Make changes incrementally and test each step.
---

### 2.4 Existing credential storage
Two existing credential systems were discovered.
**A. ModelEndpoint credentials**
The `ModelEndpoint` database model contains:
```python
api_key = Column(EncryptedText, nullable=True)
```
The `EncryptedText` type uses `src.secret_storage`.
Its encryption key is:
```text
/home/deck/OdysseusProject/data/.app_key
```
This protects database credentials at rest.
**B. API key manager**
`src/api_key_manager.py` uses a separate encrypted file:
```text
data/api_keys.json
```
Its encryption key is:
```text
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 564/588
data/.key
```
These are two existing storage systems with different purposes.
The provider integration must not introduce an unnecessary third general-purpose
secret store.
The new host-file provider credentials are a deliberately defined, separate
mechanism for managed provider keys.
### 2.5 Important encryption finding
The `DbSession.headers` database column is:
```python
headers = Column(JSON, default=dict)
```
It is ordinary JSON, not encrypted.
This is a critical security discovery.
Authentication headers containing bearer tokens or API keys must not be written
into this column.
The future architecture must resolve credentials at request time and keep host-file
secrets out of persisted session metadata.
### 2.6 Existing endpoint resolution
The existing shared resolver is:
```python
resolve_endpoint_runtime(ep, owner)
```
It currently:
- Normalizes endpoint URLs.
- Reads the endpoint's database API key.
- Resolves OAuth/session-backed credentials when `provider_auth_id` is present.
- Returns the resolved base URL and API key.
This is the natural foundation for the new provider credential resolution

The new implementation should extend this shared boundary rather than scatter

## 3. Security architecture
### 3.1 Host-side provider key storage
The agreed host-side directory structure is:
```text
/home/deck/.config/odysseus/
└── providers/
├── groq/
│ └── odysseus-groq.key
├── cloudflare/
│ └── odysseus-cloudflare.key
├── openai/
│ └── odysseus-openai.key
├── gemini/
│ └── odysseus-gemini.key
├── mistral/
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 568/588
│ └── odysseus-mistral.key
├── cerebras/
│ └── odysseus-cerebras.key
└── openrouter/
└── odysseus-openrouter.key
```
The files are stored outside the Odysseus Git repository.
Expected permissions:
- Files owned by `deck`.
- Key files mode `600`.
- Provider directories restricted appropriately.
- No API keys in source files.
- No API keys in YAML configuration.
- No API keys in logs.
- No API keys in prompts.
- No API keys exposed to browser JavaScript.
### 3.2 Container access
Verified:
- Distrobox mounts `/home/deck` into the container.
- The mount is read/write.
- The project runtime executes as UID 1000.
- The host home directory is mode `700`.
- The host `.config` directory is mode `755`.
A key file owned by `deck` with mode `600` should be readable by the application
running as that same UID.
Important limitation:
This is not a read-only mount or a separate security boundary.
The application process running as `deck` can potentially read and modify files
accessible to that user.
The design therefore relies on restrictive filesystem permissions and disciplined
application behavior, not on a claim of container-level isolation.
### 3.3 Credential resolution principle
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 569/588
The intended flow:
```text
HOST
|
| Provider key file
v
SHARED CREDENTIAL RESOLVER
|
| Resolve key at request time
v
REQUEST-SPECIFIC AUTH HEADERS
|
v
ODYSSEUS PROVIDER REQUEST
|
v
CLOUD PROVIDER
```
Credentials should be resolved only when required.
The actual secret should not be returned to frontend code or persisted in session
JSON.
### 3.4 Session protection
Session metadata should retain:
- Endpoint ID
- Endpoint URL
- Model
- Route identity
- Non-secret configuration
It should not contain host-file provider authentication headers.
Target:
```text
SESSION DATABASE
|
+-- endpoint_id
+-- endpoint_url
+-- model
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 570/588
+-- route metadata
|
+-- NO host-file API key
+-- NO persisted host-file bearer token
```
Existing custom endpoint credentials and OAuth behavior must continue to work.
Legacy session headers require a controlled compatibility and migration strategy.
Do not silently delete existing session credentials.
Do not blindly clear every session header: some existing functionality may depend
on explicitly supplied custom headers.
### 3.5 Existing modifications that must be preserved
The investigation identified existing local changes:
```text
M src/builtin_mcp.py
?? decktools/
?? requirements-odysseus.txt
```
These are not part of the provider integration and must be preserved.
Never use destructive Git cleanup or reset operations as part of this work.

7. Provider status classification
8. Sanitized logging
9. Frontend status interface
10. Provider-specific tests
Provider-specific behavior should be isolated behind this common architecture.
### 4.4 Manual provider selection
The provider selector must be manual.
Requirements:
- User explicitly selects the provider.
- User explicitly selects the model.
- No automatic provider switching.
- No automatic fallback to another cloud provider.
- No automatic paid API usage.
- A conversation should retain its selected provider unless the user changes it.
- Provider usage allowance and web-search allowance must be tracked separately.

## 5. Provider connection health
### 5.1 Required status values
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 573/588
The provider connection system must distinguish:
- `ONLINE`
- `DEGRADED`
- `OFFLINE`
- `AUTH ERROR`
- `NOT TESTED`
Rate limiting and server errors should also expose a useful reason within the
degraded state.
### 5.2 HTTP and transport classification
| Result | Classification |
|---|---|
| Authenticated HTTP 2xx | ONLINE |
| HTTP 401 | AUTH ERROR |
| HTTP 403 | AUTH ERROR |
| HTTP 429 | DEGRADED — RATE LIMITED |
| HTTP 5xx | DEGRADED — SERVER ERROR |
| DNS failure | OFFLINE |
| TLS failure | OFFLINE |
| Connection refused | OFFLINE |
| Network timeout | OFFLINE |
| No connection test performed | NOT TESTED |
A provider returning HTTP 429 is reachable.
A provider returning HTTP 5xx is reachable but experiencing a server-side problem.
Neither should be incorrectly described as a network outage.
### 5.3 Authentication must be independently verified
A model list is not proof of authentication.
A curated model fallback must never produce an ONLINE result.
A successful connection test must demonstrate that the provider accepted an
authenticated request.
Model discovery and authentication are related but separate operations.
### 5.4 Existing health system separation
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 574/588
Keep the existing general service-health vocabulary:
```text
ok
degraded
down
disabled
```
Do not silently change its meaning to the new provider-specific vocabulary.
The new provider test mechanism must be independent of the general service-health
aggregate.
The existing cache-only endpoint listing must also remain cache-only.
### 5.5 Search and grounding availability
A successful API request does not prove that web search or grounding is available.
These must be represented as separate capabilities.
Do not infer search availability from successful model authentication.
Do not infer model capabilities merely from a model ID.

## 6. Provider interface and logging requirements
### 6.1 Provider information displayed
The Command Center should eventually show:
- Provider name
- Connection status
- Credential availability
- Selected model
- Active provider
- Last connection test
- Test Connection action
- Provider-specific logs
- Useful error explanations
The interface must not display API keys.
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 575/588
### 6.2 Required log fields
Provider API logs should include, where available:
- Timestamp
- Provider
- Model
- Request ID
- HTTP status
- Latency
- Token usage
- Error category
- Sanitized error message
### 6.3 Logging security
Never log:
- API keys
- Authorization headers
- Bearer tokens
- Full secret-bearing request headers
- Secret-bearing configuration values
Technical errors should be sanitized before being shown to the user.
Authentication failures should be distinguishable from connectivity failures.
### 6.4 Frontend integration approach
Preferred order:
1. Inspect existing UI architecture.
2. Prefer existing settings and configuration mechanisms.
3. Add isolated provider dashboard components where appropriate.
4. Make isolated HTML/CSS/JavaScript changes only when required.
5. Preserve existing authentication and Content Security Policy.
6. Back up affected files before modifying them.
7. Verify reversibility and test behavior.
Avoid broad rewrites of existing application frontend files.

## 7. Proposed implementation sequence
Implementation is deliberately separate from the discovery and documentation phase.
Each step must be completed and verified before moving to the next.
### Step 1 — Host key foundation
Establish the seven-provider directory structure on the Steam Deck host.
Requirements:
- Correct ownership.
- Restrictive directory permissions.
- Key-file permissions of `600`.
- No secret values in the repository.
- No changes to Odysseus application code.
### Step 2 — Shared credential resolver
Extend the existing endpoint credential-resolution architecture.
Requirements:
- Resolve managed provider keys from host files.
- Preserve existing encrypted database credentials.
- Preserve OAuth/session-backed credential behavior.
- Preserve explicitly supplied custom endpoint credentials.
- Never expose secrets to frontend code.
- Avoid introducing another general-purpose secret store.
### Step 3 — Session credential protection
Adapt session handling so managed host-file credentials are resolved at request
time.
Requirements:
- Do not persist host-file authentication headers in `DbSession.headers`.
- Preserve endpoint identity and model selection.
- Preserve legacy custom endpoint behavior.
- Establish a controlled legacy-header compatibility strategy.
- Avoid destructive migration or silent deletion of existing credentials.
### Step 4 — Runtime integration
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 577/588
Integrate the shared resolver across the relevant request paths.
Areas to verify include:
- Main chat
- Session creation
- Session switching
- Chat route reconciliation
- Research
- Memory
- History compaction
- Compare
- Background monitoring
- Agent session tools
- Webhooks
- Model discovery and refresh
Only paths that actually require provider credentials should be changed.
Local providers and unrelated image-provider paths must remain unaffected.
### Step 5 — Groq connection testing
Implement a dedicated provider connection test.
Requirements:
- Use the host-file credential resolver.
- Do not submit the actual key through the existing form-based endpoint test.
- Do not persist the key into `ModelEndpoint.api_key`.
- Do not automatically create or assign an endpoint.
- Distinguish authentication, rate limiting, server errors, and transport failures.
- Never let curated model fallback prove authentication.
### Step 6 — Provider interface
Introduce the provider status and configuration interface.
Requirements:
- Manual provider selection.
- Manual model selection.
- Clear status information.
- Test Connection.
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 578/588
- Sanitized logs.
- No key exposure.
- No automatic fallback or switching.
### Step 7 — Verification
Verify the implementation through tests and controlled runtime checks.
Test categories:
- Credential file permissions.
- Credential resolution.
- Existing database credential compatibility.
- OAuth compatibility.
- Session persistence security.
- Request header construction.
- Chat operation.
- Model discovery.
- Connection status classification.
- Frontend behavior.
- Secret redaction.
- Existing local endpoint regression tests.
The implementation must not be considered complete merely because a model appears
in a model list.
---
## 8. Testing and acceptance criteria
The provider integration is acceptable only when all relevant criteria are met.
### Credential security
- [ ] Host keys remain outside the Git repository.
- [ ] Key files are owned by `deck`.
- [ ] Key files use mode `600`.
- [ ] Secrets do not appear in logs.
- [ ] Secrets do not appear in browser responses.
- [ ] Host-file credentials are not persisted in session JSON.
- [ ] Existing encrypted endpoint credentials still work.
- [ ] Existing OAuth/session credentials still work.
### Provider connection
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 579/588
- [ ] Valid authenticated request produces ONLINE.
- [ ] HTTP 401 produces AUTH ERROR.
- [ ] HTTP 403 produces AUTH ERROR.
- [ ] HTTP 429 produces DEGRADED — RATE LIMITED.
- [ ] HTTP 5xx produces DEGRADED — SERVER ERROR.
- [ ] DNS/TLS/refused connection produces OFFLINE.
- [ ] Untested provider produces NOT TESTED.
- [ ] Curated model fallback cannot produce ONLINE.
### Runtime integration
- [ ] Main chat uses the shared resolver.
- [ ] Session switching uses the shared resolver.
- [ ] Research and memory paths remain functional.
- [ ] Background and agent paths remain functional.
- [ ] Existing local providers remain functional.
- [ ] Existing custom endpoint credentials remain functional.
- [ ] Existing endpoint inventory remains cache-only.
### User interface
- [ ] Provider status is understandable.
- [ ] Model selection is manual.
- [ ] Provider selection is manual.
- [ ] Test Connection works.
- [ ] Logs are sanitized.
- [ ] No automatic provider switching occurs.
- [ ] No automatic paid API usage occurs.

Once the common architecture is proven with Groq, the remaining providers should be
onboarded one at a time.
For every new provider:
1. Confirm its official API base URL.
2. Confirm authentication requirements.
3. Confirm model discovery behavior.
4. Confirm request and streaming compatibility.
5. Confirm provider-specific error behavior.
6. Add provider configuration.
7. Add provider-specific tests.

9. Verify secret handling.
10. Verify manual selection and model behavior.
Do not assume that every provider supports identical request parameters, streaming
behavior, model discovery, tools, or search capabilities.
The common architecture should provide shared infrastructure without pretending
provider capabilities are identical.

## 10. Protected project boundaries
The following rules apply throughout implementation.
- Do not delete or reset existing project changes.
- Preserve the existing `src/builtin_mcp.py` modification.
- Preserve `decktools/`.
- Preserve `requirements-odysseus.txt`.
- Do not replace existing endpoint systems unnecessarily.
- Do not modify unrelated Pi-hole, SSH, Stable Diffusion, or Local AI projects.
- Do not enable automatic startup.
- Do not introduce automatic provider fallback.
- Do not expose API keys to the browser.
- Do not perform destructive database migrations without explicit review.
- Back up affected files before modifications.
- Verify each change before proceeding.

## 12. Architectural decision record
The following decisions are the current agreed direction.
**ADR-001 — One shared provider architecture**
Build common provider infrastructure once for seven providers. Groq is the first
implementation.
**ADR-002 — Host-side managed credentials**
Managed provider keys live outside the project repository under:
```text
/home/deck/.config/odysseus/providers/
```
**ADR-003 — Request-time credential resolution**
Resolve host-file credentials at the request boundary. Do not store them in
unencrypted session headers.
**ADR-004 — Preserve existing credential mechanisms**
Existing encrypted database credentials and OAuth/session-backed credentials must
continue working.
**ADR-005 — Separate inventory from health**
Cached model inventory is not live provider connection health.
**ADR-006 — Authentication must be proven**
10/31/26, 2:18 AM *1.1* Odysseus - Groq Step Inspection
https://chatgpt.com/c/6ae4a773-eefc-83ed-8b31-2030acc8ce24 582/588
Curated models and successful model-list fallback cannot prove API authentication.
**ADR-007 — Explicit provider health states**
Use ONLINE, DEGRADED, OFFLINE, AUTH ERROR, and NOT TESTED, with meaningful reason
classifications.
**ADR-008 — Manual provider selection**
No automatic switching, fallback, or paid usage.
**ADR-009 — Search is a separate capability**
API connectivity does not establish web-search or grounding availability.
**ADR-010 — Incremental implementation**
One small, verified implementation step at a time.
---
## 13. Final note
This document is the architectural reference for the Odysseus multi-provider
integration.
It records what was discovered, why the existing architecture matters, what
security risks were identified, and what the implementation must preserve.
Future implementation changes should be compared against this document.
If the architecture changes, update this document deliberately and record the
reason.
**The guiding principle: understand first, design once, implement carefully, verify
everything.**
---
**End of document.**
